shadow-lg">@Ljh684/moonnet-lab/src/sim.<span class="text-blue-900 dark:text-indigo-300">Sim</span></div></div><span class="text-blue-900 dark:text-indigo-300">Sim</span></span></a><span class="text-zinc-500 dark:text-gray-400 text-nowrap">,</span><span> </span><span class="text-cyan-800 dark:text-sky-300">tag</span><span> </span><span class="text-zinc-500 dark:text-gray-400 mt-3"><div class="pl-4 mb-2"><div class="text-sm md:text-base leading-relaxed text-gray-800 dark:text-slate-200"><span><p><!--[--><!--]--></p><div class="mb-4 md:pr-8"><span><span class="inline">Decide whether the transmission that just started is lost.</span></span></div><p><!--[--><!--]--></p><div class="mb-4 md:pr-8"><span><span class="inline">A packet that carries a tag is judged on its identity: the same tag on the</span>
<span class="inline">same link always meets the same fate, whatever else was transmitted in</span>
<span class="inline">between. That is what lets two senders be compared on the same lost packets</span>
<span class="inline">rather than merely on the same conditions.</span></span></div><p><!--[--><!--]--></p><div class="mb-4 md:pr-8"><span><span class="inline">A packet with no tag — an acknowledgment, whose identity is not stable</span>
<span class="inline">because the same acknowledgment is sent repeatedly — falls back to drawing</span>
<span class="inline">from the link's own stream. Its loss pattern is therefore still a property of</span>
<span class="inline">transmission order, which is worth knowing when reading a comparison: the</span>
<span class="inline">data loss is controlled, the acknowledgment loss is not.</span></span></div><p><!--[--><!--]--></p><div class="mb-4 md:pr-8"><span><span class="inline">The draw is skipped entirely on a loss-free link, so adding a reliable link</span>
<span class="inline">to a scenario cannot shift the random stream of the links around it.</span></span></div></span></div></div></div></div><div class="mb-3 group mt-10"><div class="flex items-center <span class="inline">headers: that is the quantity that costs serialization time, and getting it</span>
<span class="inline">right is what makes throughput numbers meaningful.</span></span></div><p><!--[--><!--]--></p><div class="mb-4 md:pr-8"><span><span class="inline">The payload type is a parameter so that the TCP layer can hang its segment</span>

packet.</span></span></div><p><!--[--><!--]--></p><div class="mb-4 md:pr-8"><span><span class="font-mono bg-zinc-200 dark:bg-gray-900 text-gray-800 dark:text-slate-200 text-[0.9em] px-[5px] py-[2px] rounded">tag</span><span class="inline"> is the packet's identity: a number the sender derives from what the</span>
<span class="inline">packet </span><em><span class="inline">is</span></em><span class="inline"> rather than from when it was sent. A link decides loss from the</span>
<span class="inline">tag, so the same packet meets the same fate on the same link whatever else is</span>
<span class="inline">in flight — which is what turns a comparison between two senders into a</span>
<span class="inline">controlled experiment. Zero means "no identity", and the link falls back to</span>
<span class="inline">drawing from its own stream.</span></span></div></span></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div class="mb-3 group mt-10"><div class="flex items-center justify-between
